lifetimes; Smoke proves cancelled churn and the directed-signal/readiness race.

and stopped-guest refusal must satisfy those issues before Gate 14 changes.

do not override this summary or relabel their original evidence.

## Product scenario interpretation

The [product scenarios](workload-profiles.md#product-scenarios-and-verification)
define the evidence needed to claim static-file/client service, persistent
uploads, SQLite recovery and process isolation. Pair focused verification with
original end-to-end guest results, including the relevant failure/recovery path.
Host check counts, compiler inspection and replay are supporting evidence, not
substitutes for those results. General hosting also requires independent apps
and changed rebuilds through unchanged kernel/EFI and deployment tools, without
per-app manual disassembly or OS patches. These requirements apply to the named
successor capabilities; they do not change historical receipts or add all four
scenarios to the existing finite #226/#227 local Kestrel gate.

Completion of a finite gate is a development milestone, not completion of the
OS product. The integrated target is continuing Minimal API and persistent
SQLite in the same guest, serving a Blazor Static WebAssembly frontend to a
browser that calls the API. Restart and recovery are required. Finite test
timeouts/request counts must remain test controls rather than the normal workload
lifetime. Qualification must exercise sustained service and recovery without
installing a test termination condition in the normal application.
The planned live cloud demo additionally requires provider qualification.

## Architecture review acceptance boundaries

The [October 16 ordered follow-ups](workload-profiles.md#architecture-review-implementation-order-2026-10-16)
change prerequisites, not gate results. Step1351 retains its finite socket
package scope; step1380 adds bounded configuration and M07-027/M07-028 repair
qualification; step1389 adds finite #218 copied logging/quota delivery, and
step1405 adds bounded #219 startup/#745 vectors. Remaining service prerequisites
stay open.

- Step1419 qualifies #220's bounded adversarial socket ladder, #221 policy and
  #222 exact health/stopped-guest refusal, preserving the prerequisite scopes.
- Step1419 also qualifies #759/#223 owned realtime and independently plausible
  HTTP Date. A correctly formatted Unix-epoch response is rejected; the declared
  UTC source, tolerance and finite lifetime remain restrictions.
- Step1437 qualifies #224's persist, compact TIME-WAIT, continuing execution,
  replenished randomness, pending-reset, continuing-ISN and empty-send
  prerequisites: one complete service lifetime plus ten affected ancestors and
  5,809 observed host markers. M11-026/#784 found13 unawaited async cases:
  full-host qualification is withheld pending a corrected unfiltered run.
  Independent original guest/native receipts remain retained. #224 additionally
  requires the harness repair and repeated-service acceptance.
  #225 additionally requires fragmented backing, guest-verified GC/native
  budgets and M16-020 pending-signal checks before renewed waits. The existing
  #226 -> #227 evidence and local Gate 14 closure chain remains authoritative.
- M16-017 requires real CPL3 #UD/#DE capture/termination and supervisor progress;
  #298 still needs a second protected app serving through that failure. Host
  models or a single restored supervisor do not qualify multi-app isolation.
- M16-021 requires accepted SIGPIPE dispositions to agree with I/O behavior,
  or be explicitly restricted, before #292 general process termination acceptance.
- Roslyn checks, generated ABI/capability declarations and benchmark tooling
  remain future work. Native/guest qualification is required when emitted kernel
  code or its build closure changes; a generator pass is not a safety proof.

The first pass reproduced two host failures and characterized fragmentation.
The second adds four expected host failures, a realtime characterization and
independent reinspection of retained PR739 development packets carrying epoch
dates. Neither pass ran new full/native/QEMU qualification or implemented these
repairs. Preserve failed reproductions and prior accepted finite receipts. No
numbered, cloud or production gate advances from this review.

The [workload decision](workload-profiles.md) changes implementation order,
not evidence status. M16 qualifies the Linux ELF/upstream NativeAOT foundation;
M10 then qualifies local `SingleMinimalApi` Kestrel; M14 qualifies two isolated
local apps before AWS/Azure. CoreCLR is a later independent hosted target.
General custom kernel EH/Gate4 is not a blanket dependency for the separate
upstream application runtime. Kernel paths still require their own supported
runtime operations. No existing gate changes to Pass in this migration.
Historical CoreCLR/TPA wording below applies to that future route; NativeAOT
acceptance must satisfy the explicit first-web contract in workload profiles.

Gate status is explicit:

- `Pass` means every acceptance item for that gate has preserved evidence.
- `Fail` means at least one required acceptance item is missing.
- `Unknown` means the repository lacks enough evidence to classify the gate.

Only Gate 2 currently passes, and that pass is limited to the documented
firmware-visible QEMU/OVMF boot-smoke boundary. It is not `ExitBootServices`,
owned-driver, storage, networking, app-launch, hosted-.NET, Kestrel, cloud, or
production evidence.

## Gate 14 Profile Split

Kestrel support is mandatory for the CloudReady workload milestone. The
evidence needed to claim it is profile-specific:

- `SingleMinimalApi` is the near-term CloudReady gate. It can pass with one
  packaged ASP.NET Minimal API workload when the guest starts the documented
  .NET 11 NativeAOT payload or a separately qualified future hosted runtime,
  loads package/config data, supplies the required file, configuration, socket,
  timer, thread and synchronization services, binds Kestrel to the manifest
  endpoint, and an external runner receives the expected
  `GET /healthz` response over the OS network stack. Required serial/runner
  evidence includes the normal boot markers plus `TEST_START kestrel.smoke`,
  `KESTREL_PACKAGE_CONTRACT kestrel.smoke.package.v0`, `KESTREL_APP_START`,
  `KESTREL_BIND_READY`, `HTTP_PROBE_START`, `HTTP_PROBE_PASS`, and
  `TEST_PASS kestrel.smoke`. The [NativeAOT-first route](workload-profiles.md)
  uses the restricted Linux x64 syscall adapter and does not require CoreCLR
  or exported `OSHost_*` PAL functions. A future hosted-runtime route must
  qualify its required `OSHost_*` contracts separately. This route selection
  does not relax the real guest, network, lifecycle or security evidence.
- `SingleMinimalApi` does not require a process table, process IDs,
  per-process address spaces, a multi-app supervisor, per-app resource limits,
  or a two-app crash-isolation proof. It also does not make a multi-app
  containment claim.
- `MultiMinimalApi` is a later gate. It requires all `SingleMinimalApi` Kestrel
  evidence plus at least two ASP.NET Minimal API workloads launched from
  manifest data, distinct app lifecycle identity, distinct listen endpoints,
  per-app config/environment/log streams, process or equivalent address-space
  isolation, supervisor start/stop/restart policy, per-app exit status capture,
  port-conflict handling, and crash-isolation evidence proving one failed app
  does not corrupt the OS or prevent the surviving app from continuing to serve
  HTTP responses.

Both profile gates are currently `Fail`. Host-only fixture builds, dry-run HTTP
probe text, mock PAL tables, native-tier HTTP demos, and documentation updates
are not Kestrel evidence for either profile.

### Gate 14 Provider Split

The `SingleMinimalApi` Kestrel gate is also provider-scoped:

- QEMU/libvirt Kestrel claim: requires the guest to serve the
  `kestrel.smoke.package.v0` `GET /healthz` response through the local
  QEMU/libvirt network path with the required guest serial and runner markers.
- AWS Nitro Kestrel claim: requires the same guest Kestrel markers on EC2 plus
  Nitro-specific ENA networking, NVMe root storage, DHCP IPv4, TCP listener
  behavior, EC2 image/AMI evidence, and EC2 serial-log evidence. AWS IMDSv2,
  user-data, cloud-init-free docs, and local QEMU virtio evidence are not AWS
  Kestrel proof.
- Azure VM Kestrel claim: requires the same guest Kestrel markers on Azure plus
  Hyper-V detection, VMBus, NetVSC networking, StorVSC storage, DHCP IPv4, TCP
  listener behavior, fixed-VHD/image evidence, and Azure serial or boot
  diagnostic evidence. Azure WireServer/goal-state fixtures and local QEMU
  virtio evidence are not Azure Kestrel proof.

`MultiMinimalApi` repeats the provider split and adds multi-app process,
isolation, supervisor, restart, port-conflict, and crash-isolation evidence.

  storage, and keeps record/result layouts free of managed references.

  `BootEventRecord` snapshot lines into `PanicTextBuffer` or caller-owned byte

  rejects default/zero formatter results as successful evidence.

  register dump through `PanicSerialReporter`, report invalid frame and reporter
  failures, and drive `BootSequence.RunCpuFaultPanicReadiness` ready/blocked
  markers while leaving the default boot path unchanged.

  caller-owned byte buffers, reports destination capacity, rejects invalid

  evidence.

### M02-014 terminal fault-transition host boundary

Step936 adds the reviewed #513 host-only transition emitter over the #510
table-image contract. Independent byte/interpreter tests and LLVM decoding
cover the 361-instruction stream, early CR2/frame capture, bounded page walks
and finite serial output. The actual 17-source no-stdlib closure has zero
assembly references. Fresh parent focused checks pass. Step939 later adds
complete host integration; step944 adds the separate default-off adapter's
actual default/enabled guest evidence.

No generated instruction is executed by this leaf API itself. The later
adapter freshly audits exact backing, publishes the closed stream and captures
the bounded terminal fault. Gate 3 remains **Fail**. Modeled bad-CPL checks do
not establish unprivileged recovery; active diagnostics require the trusted
CPL0 boundary.

### M04-004 arithmetic conformance boundary

The fixed M04-004 catalog executes 57 vectors across 30 integer arithmetic,
shift, cast, numeric enum, and 64-bit divide/remainder operations. Its host
tests use an independent arbitrary-precision oracle for 6,300 combinations,
check rejection/default behavior and zero allocation, and compile the same
source without framework assembly references. Catalog fingerprint:
`45FDFA24DD72EA28`. The exact host marker explicitly carries
`hostConformance=true guestIntegrated=false gate4=Fail`.

The separate #507 adapter now links the unchanged catalog through a default-off
68-compile-item/81-source-input EFI profile, explicitly requiring M04-002 and
M04-003. No boot phase is added. Actual-shim metadata, controlled adapter
rejection, default/enabled publish, and same-build artifact checks pass; step928
records those boundaries. Step929 adds the complete host gate and independently
reviewed fresh default/enabled QEMU/OVMF tuple at source
`07da84eb0305b680d43f72f4fd690d5ed6fe3e18`. The enabled guest executes the fixed
57-vector catalog and emits fingerprint `45FDFA24DD72EA28` once, after M04-003
and before memory-map capture, with no failure marker. The default emits no M04
records. Exact firmware-return output is distinguished from the clean CLROS
marker interval; raw runner noise counters are not represented as zero.
This establishes the scoped NativeAOT guest catalog only. Gate 4 remains
**Fail**; CLR exception delivery and runtime completeness remain unsupported.
See `docs/nativeaot-nostdlib-limits.md` for supported operations, the no-throw
rejection policy, and reference provenance.

### M04-005 layout contract boundary

The source-pinned `ObjectArrayStringEETypeMethodTableLayoutContracts` now
evaluates object/array/string size and offset metadata plus the fixed
24-byte MethodTable prefix for NativeAOT 10.0.8, x64 little-endian. Independent
literal/byte fixtures, adversarial status cases, zero-allocation checks and
actual-source no-stdlib compilation pass. Step931 records this host evidence
and its exact source identities. Supplemental #512 now integrates the frozen
evaluator into a default-off EFI adapter with 19 exact scalar result checks,
the explicit 69/82 source closure and strict whole-transcript validation.
Step932 records reviewed integration and the previous full run's manifest
ownership-note failure, corrected without weakening its test. Step933 records
accepted complete host execution (3,527 starts, 3,528 passes, zero failures,
exit 0) and fresh default/layout QEMU at source
`5f6108ca363f03c7ebad8dafca7ae78e1d3a0db2`. The enabled guest emits the exact
19-check result once in order; default emits no M04 records. Independent review
verifies source/package authority, serial boundaries and embedded EFI payloads.
The separately tracked #514 FAT geometry defect prevents any whole-filesystem
claim for these images. Closure follows reviewed merge and tracker evidence.

Gate 4 remains **Fail**. Neither this contract nor later execution of its
value checks establishes compiler-emitted physical object layout, live heap
allocation, GC, metadata discovery, optional-tail access, dispatch,
exception/unwind support, real `ExitBootServices` or hosted .NET/Kestrel.

### M04-006 heap-page integration boundary

Step935 adds the reviewed `ManagedHeapPageRangeLiveAllocator` host contract:
one actual caller-mapped page is reserved through the original allocator,

## Unsupported Behavior

Hardware fault diagnostics, allocator-owned mutable free-list installation from compact boot regions, complete UEFI encapsulation beyond the bounded M03-007 exit, reusable owned serial/framebuffer, runtime helpers, storage, networking, app loading, socket bind/listen behavior, exported PAL socket/timer/thread/file contracts, hosted .NET 10, Kestrel, ISO generation, libvirt real boot, provider cloud import, CI, and hardening remain incomplete. Libvirt lifecycle planning and check-serial validation are host-only contract evidence; they do not connect to libvirt, start a VM, poll live serial output, or prove cleanup against a live domain.

`SingleMinimalApi` and `MultiMinimalApi` are not complete. `SingleMinimalApi` still requires the hosted runtime or app-target boundary, live package/config access, socket/timer/thread prerequisites, Kestrel bind, an external HTTP probe, and serial evidence. `MultiMinimalApi` additionally requires process tables, per-app address spaces, supervisor/restart policy, and crash isolation.

## Known Limitations

Do not describe the OS as complete, production-ready, or cloud-ready until every applicable gate in this ledger has pass evidence.

The current default QEMU pass is an EFI smoke with ordered boot markers, UEFI
memory-map capture, and compact boot memory-region materialization. The separate
opt-in M01-026 pass adds only its exact reversible pre-EBS owned-root and scoped
protection/page-zero observations. Neither is an `ExitBootServices`, owned
diagnostics/driver, storage, network, or hosted-workload proof.

The separate M02-010 pass adds only one pre-`ExitBootServices` temporary
LoaderData allocation, exact 4,096-byte materialization and production
readback, deterministic modeled relocation, cleanup, and serial-marker proof.
The common target remains address metadata and no generated slot was executed.
This is not executable-publication, W^X/NX, IDT/IDTR/IST, live interrupt,
normalized CPU-frame, fault-handler, or panic-routing evidence. Gate 3 remains
`Fail`; every numbered gate state is unchanged and Gate 2 remains the only
passing gate.

The host-friendly caller-owned `MemoryMapInfo` region writer, allocator-facing
`BootMemoryRegionPlanner`, free-list `BootPageAllocatorPlanner`, caller-owned
`BootPageFreeListBuilder`, `BootPageAllocatorStateInitializer`, caller-owned
`BootPageAllocatorMutation`, `BootPageAllocatorReadinessComposer`,
`NativeAotHeapBootstrapPlanner`, and
`BootSequence.RunBootPageAllocatorReadiness` marker gate are still host-tested
against synthetic descriptors or compact records and caller-provided storage.
Default live EFI uses the no-stdlib `BootMemoryRegionMaterializer` to write
compact `BootMemoryRegionRecord` entries and emits `BOOT_PAGE_ALLOCATOR_READY`
with live conventional/free node/page/byte counts, but it does not run the
host-friendly allocator-facing planning chain, expose page allocation/free
APIs, reserve managed heap pages, configure paging, or enforce NX/W^X. The
separate M01-026 all-switch tuple allocates only its explicit arenas and one
metadata page, constructs and temporarily enters only its fixed owned-root
profile, and restores the exact cycle. It does not establish a general
allocator, permanent paging authority, managed heap, or systemwide W^X.

`BOOT_HANDOFF_READY`, `UEFI_MEMORY_MAP_NORMALIZED`,
`BOOT_MEMORY_MAP_ACCEPTED`, `BOOT_MEMORY_REGIONS_READY`,
`BOOT_MEMORY_REGION_COUNT`, `BOOT_PAGE_ALLOCATOR_READY`, and
`BOOT_ACPI_RSDP_HANDOFF_READY` are QEMU-proven from the live default EFI
artifact. The five ACPI physical discovery markers are QEMU-proven only from
the explicit opt-in probe artifact. The complete M01 stage-one-through-stage-three
marker chain and `CLROS_M01_LIVE_BOOT_MEMORY_READY` are QEMU-proven only
from the source-bound M01-026 all-switch artifact. `UefiExitBootServicesPlan`
and `PlatformFirmwareExitBootServicesResult` are host-tested only and do not
prove real firmware shutdown. None of this proves owned drivers, storage,
networking, or hosted workload launch.

`EnableExitBootServicesFreshMapProbe` is disabled by default. Its explicit EFI probe build reacquires and validates a fresh memory map and then emits `UEFI_EBS_CALL_SKIPPED`; it is not a live firmware `ExitBootServices` call.

`EfiConfigurationTableScanner` is host-tested and QEMU-marker-tested, and its selected pointer is now carried through the typed handoff as provenance. The live EFI path still does not parse or dereference the RSDP. `AcpiRsdpValidator` is host-tested only against caller-provided byte buffers.

`AcpiSdtParser` is host-tested parser evidence, and the explicit ACPI probe has live pre-`ExitBootServices` root SDT validation evidence on QEMU/OVMF. The default smoke path does not walk ACPI tables, and none of this proves post-firmware physical-memory access.

`AcpiMcfgParser` is host-tested parser evidence, and the explicit ACPI probe has live pre-`ExitBootServices` MCFG discovery/checksum/segment-parse evidence on QEMU/OVMF. It still does not map ECAM MMIO, enumerate PCI devices, size BARs, or bind drivers.

`PciConfigurationHeader` is host-tested byte-buffer parser evidence only. It consumes caller-provided first-64-byte type-0 PCI configuration headers, records raw BAR values, and validates only the capabilities-list pointer byte when advertised by the status register. It does not scan ECAM, read or write PCI config space, walk capability structures, size/map BARs, configure MSI/MSI-X, bind drivers, or prove virtio device discovery from live hardware.

`PciBarDecoder`, `PciBarSizingPlanner`, `PciBarSizingProbePlanner`, `PciBarSizingProbeAddressPlanner`, `PciBarSizingProbeExecutor`, and `PciBarSizingProbeDerivedSizePlanner` are host-tested metadata/test-double evidence only. The decoder consumes raw BAR values from an already parsed type-0 header and returns read-only descriptors for absent, I/O, 32-bit MMIO, 64-bit MMIO, consumed, and malformed slots. The sizing planner consumes those descriptors plus caller-owned all-ones sizing readback snapshots to derive aperture sizes for later validators. Default/zero decode, sizing plan, sizing-probe plan, sizing-probe address-plan, sizing-probe execution, config-read, and derived-size values are explicitly not successful until initialized by the relevant planner or accessor. The probe planner records the command sequence a future PCI config-space accessor must execute to write all ones, capture sizing readbacks, and restore original BAR values, including 64-bit low/high BAR pairs. The address planner binds each planned command to checked ECAM config-space address metadata for caller-provided segment and bus/device/function metadata. The fake executor calls only an injected config-accessor test double and writes caller-owned readback snapshots. The derived-size composer feeds those fake-captured readbacks into `PciBarSizingPlanner` and preserves nested status provenance. It does not execute live config-space writes, execute live config-space reads, restore BARs on hardware, validate that a live device accepted the probe, map ECAM or BARs, allocate pages, configure MSI/MSI-X, bind drivers, or prove live PCI enumeration.

`VirtioPciBindingDerivedBarSizePlanner` is host-tested metadata/test-double composition only. It consumes caller-provided PCI configuration bytes and BAR sizes produced from fake-captured sizing readbacks, then invokes the existing virtio PCI binding plan. Default/zero derived-binding plan values are explicitly not successful until initialized by the planner. The BAR sizes carry fake-executor provenance, not live hardware provenance. It does not execute live PCI config-space reads or writes, restore BARs on hardware, validate that a device accepted an all-ones sizing probe, map ECAM or BARs, perform volatile MMIO, allocate or map DMA memory, bind drivers, perform packet or block I/O, or prove QEMU/cloud, hosted .NET 10, ASP.NET Minimal API, or Kestrel readiness.

`VirtioPciBindingDerivedBarSizeCatalog` is host-tested metadata/test-double composition only. It consumes caller-provided candidates and PCI configuration snapshots plus an injected fake config accessor and caller-owned scratch buffers, then invokes the fake-derived binding planner per candidate. Default/zero catalog result values are explicitly not successful until initialized by the catalog builder. The catalog preserves fake-executor provenance, not live hardware provenance. It does not enumerate live PCI devices, execute live PCI config-space reads or writes, restore BARs on hardware, validate that a device accepted an all-ones sizing probe, map ECAM or BARs, perform volatile MMIO, allocate or map DMA memory, bind drivers, perform packet or block I/O, or prove QEMU/cloud, hosted .NET 10, ASP.NET Minimal API, or Kestrel readiness.

`PciBusScanner` is host-tested injected-reader evidence only. It coordinates bounded PCI bus/device/function scanning over caller-provided first-64-byte config-space snapshots, but the reader is still a test double or future platform boundary. It does not discover MCFG tables, map ECAM, read live MMIO, size or map BARs, walk capability lists, configure interrupts, bind drivers, or prove hardware enumeration under QEMU.

`PciEcamPhysicalHeaderReader` is host-tested injected-physical-memory evidence only. It plans an ECAM address and requests exactly the first 64 bytes of a function's configuration space through `IPlatformPhysicalMemory`, then adapts that result into the existing PCI bus scanner delegate. It does not implement a real physical-memory mapper, configure cache/MMIO attributes, execute live ECAM reads, enumerate QEMU/cloud hardware, write configuration space, size/map BARs, walk capabilities, configure interrupts, or bind drivers.

`PhysicalMmioOwnershipPlan` is host-tested planning metadata only. It does not reserve ECAM ranges in live boot-memory ownership state, configure page tables, set cacheability attributes, map ECAM, read or write PCI configuration space, enumerate PCI, bind virtio devices, or prove cloud-provider hardware support.

It does not prove native app argument-vector materialization, app ABI execution, hosted .NET, Minimal API, or Kestrel unless a real guest image emits `APP_ARGS_VECTOR_READY` from the tested boot path.

`dotnet run --project tools/OS.Tooling -- qemu smoke --marker-preset cctor-preflight` is a dry-run and real-run marker requirement selector only. It does not prove class constructors execute, static reference fields are fully supported, exception unwinding works, or NativeAOT runtime correctness is complete unless a real guest image emits `NATIVEAOT_CCTOR_RUNNER_PREFLIGHT_READY` from the tested boot path.

`dotnet run --project tools/OS.Tooling -- qemu smoke --marker-preset fetch-http` is a dry-run and real-run marker requirement selector only. It does not prove live FetchApp execution, virtio-net, DHCP, DNS, TCP, HTTP GET, hosted .NET, Kestrel, or app completion unless a real guest image emits `FETCH_APP_HTTP_COMPLETION_READY` from the tested boot path.

`dotnet run --project tools/OS.Tooling -- qemu smoke --marker-preset fetch-http-endpoint` is a dry-run and real-run marker requirement selector only. It does not prove live FetchApp execution, manifest loading from disk, virtio-net, DHCP, DNS, TCP, HTTP GET, hosted .NET, Kestrel, or endpoint use unless a real guest image emits `FETCHAPP_HTTP_ENDPOINT_READY` from the tested boot path.

`dotnet run --project tools/OS.Tooling -- qemu smoke --marker-preset http-static-response` is a dry-run and real-run marker requirement selector only. It does not prove live HTTP server behavior, sockets, hosted .NET, Minimal API, Kestrel, cloud readiness, or production readiness unless a real guest image emits `HTTP_STATIC_RESPONSE_READY` and `HTTP_STATIC_RESPONSE` from the tested boot path.

`dotnet run --project tools/OS.Tooling -- qemu smoke --marker-preset http-static-tcp-response` is a dry-run and real-run marker requirement selector only. It does not prove live HTTP server behavior, sockets, hosted .NET, Minimal API, Kestrel, cloud readiness, or production readiness unless a real guest image emits `HTTP_STATIC_TCP_RESPONSE_READY` and `HTTP_STATIC_TCP_RESPONSE` from the tested boot path.

`dotnet run --project tools/OS.Tooling -- qemu smoke --marker-preset virtio-rx-tcp-dispatch` is a dry-run and real-run marker requirement selector only. It does not prove live virtio-net RX queue completion, packet I/O, TCP delivery, sockets, hosted .NET, Minimal API, or Kestrel unless a real guest image emits `VIRTIO_NET_RX_TCP_DISPATCH_READY` and `VIRTIO_NET_RX_TCP_DISPATCH` from the tested boot path.

`dotnet run --project tools/OS.Tooling -- qemu smoke --marker-preset acpi` by itself is an opt-in marker requirement only. Paired with the explicit ACPI probe artifact and passing QEMU serial log it proves only pre-`ExitBootServices` RSDP/root SDT/MCFG byte discovery. It does not prove ECAM mapping, PCI enumeration, driver binding, real `ExitBootServices`, or post-firmware owned serial output. `--marker-preset diagnostics` is a dry-run/marker requirement for the host-only diagnostics readiness chain and does not prove live IDT or panic behavior. `--marker-preset uefi-gone` remains a marker requirement for simulation only.

`BootSequence.RunAppPayloadReadiness` and `APP_PAYLOAD_READY` are managed host-test planning only. They do not prove QEMU boot-time package loading, complete executable-format validation, executable mapping, native app execution, hosted .NET 10, Minimal API, or Kestrel behavior.

`BootSequence.RunAppExecutableMappingReadiness` and `APP_EXEC_MAPPING_READY` are managed host-test planning only. They do not prove QEMU boot-time package loading, executable page allocation, W^X/NX enforcement, relocation, native app execution, hosted .NET 10, Minimal API, or Kestrel behavior.

`BootSequence.RunAppExecutableMemoryReadiness` and `APP_EXEC_MEMORY_READY` are managed host-test planning only. They do not prove QEMU boot-time package loading, executable page allocation, live W^X/NX enforcement, payload copying, relocation, native app execution, hosted .NET 10, Minimal API, or Kestrel behavior.

`BootSequence.RunAppInvocationReadiness` and `APP_INVOCATION_READY` are managed host-test planning only. They do not prove QEMU boot-time package loading, stack allocation, stack switching, thread creation, final app ABI, page mapping, relocation, native app execution, hosted .NET 10, Minimal API, or Kestrel behavior.

`BootSequence.RunAppLifecycleReadiness` and `APP_LIFECYCLE_READY` are managed host-test planning only. They do not prove QEMU boot-time package loading, app supervisor state, live stdout/stderr routing, exit-code capture, restart policy, native app execution, hosted .NET 10, Minimal API, or Kestrel behavior.

`NativeAppSelectionBootScratchPlan`, `NativeAppSelectionLoader`, `VirtioBlockNativeAppSelectionPreflight`, `BootSequence.RunAppSelectionReadiness`, `BootSequence.RunAppSelectionPreflightReadiness`, `APP_SELECTION_READY`, and `APP_SELECTION_PREFLIGHT_READY` are managed host-test planning only. They do not prove boot-owned allocation, live virtio-blk reads, QEMU boot-time package loading, executable mapping, native app execution, hosted .NET 10, Minimal API, or Kestrel behavior.

`BootSequence.RunAppEntryReadiness` and `APP_ENTRY_READY` are managed host-test planning only. They do not prove QEMU boot-time package loading, executable page allocation, stack allocation, stack switching, final app ABI dispatch, page mapping, relocation, native app execution, hosted .NET 10, Minimal API, or Kestrel behavior.

Kernel-facing `app.kind=package`, `runtime.kind=native-aot`, `env.KEY=value`, and `listen=host:port` manifest parsing plus launch-plan selection, bind-policy validation, mock bind-bridge composition, mock listen-bridge composition, and host-only config-table projection prove metadata/mock-table handling only. Environment/listen fields are not exported PAL environment/config queries, hosted .NET configuration, ASP.NET configuration, or Kestrel binding. Listen endpoints, bind-policy checks, mock table binds, and mock table listen state are not exported socket binds/listens, real TCP listeners, hosted .NET configuration, ASP.NET configuration, or Kestrel evidence. Rejected hosted runtime values such as `runtime.kind=dotnet10` are not hosted .NET or Kestrel evidence.

The `kestrel.smoke.package.v0` contract is documentation evidence only. It defines future package files, manifest fields, `OS_SMOKE_EXPECTED_BODY=OS_MINIMAL_API_OK`, `GET /healthz`, `HTTP_PROBE_PASS`, and `TEST_PASS kestrel.smoke` requirements, but it does not prove hosted runtime launch, exported PAL file/config/socket/timer/thread functions, TCP listener support, runner HTTP probing, or Kestrel.
